```text
Summary 8 tests run: 8 passed, 0 skipped
```

## B6: payload effects, snapshot replies, backend-owned outcomes (BX28, G11, G12, F38)

Plan: design v7.1 sections 2.3, 5.1 and 5.2 and section 7 (B6), test plan v7.1 T38 BL with its review verdict (GO). Tests: `apps/jackin/tests/effects.rs`, feature `demo`. The file grows one slice per commit; each slice records its red here before its code. A red run uses a type scaffold only: the new `Effect` and `Msg` shapes and the `Completion::ready` constructor, with the screens and the sim still behaving as before, because the tests do not compile without the types and a compile error is not a disagreement. The scaffold lives in a scratch worktree at `900e06f` (and, for later slices, at the previous slice's commit); it is never committed.

Decisions made while building (they hold for every slice):

- `Reply` and `Backend::settle`. A backend answers an effect with `Completion::ready(after_ms, msg)` (a reply it has built) or `Completion::settle(after_ms)`; for the second, `World` keeps the effect as a `Work::Settle` job and calls `Backend::settle(&effect, &world)` when the job falls due. The simulator settles every reply that carries a `Snapshot`, because the old screens applied their changes to the world as it was at reply time. A snapshot built when the effect starts would undo a change another reply made in between (two stops 500 ms apart: the first reply would put the second instance back).
- Replies with no delay are delivered at once. `App::handle_input` and the tick call `World::take_due` after the input, so an effect the sim answers with `after_ms = 0` finishes before the next frame, also under a paused clock. This keeps the saves that were synchronous (accounts) synchronous, with no frame in between and no `N working` strip.
- `sim::snapshot(&World) -> Snapshot` is the simulator's one way to build a reply snapshot. It carries `index_error` from the arbiter, so applying it leaves an unreadable index unreadable.
- `next_workspace_id` is gone from `World` and `WorldParts`. `SimBackend` owns the counter (starts at 100 like the demo worlds) and hands out `max(counter, highest id + 1)`; the rule that `World::apply` used to keep moves with it (`tests/snapshot.rs::the_sim_never_hands_out_a_workspace_id_in_use`, the old test rewritten against the sim).
- The wizard builds its workspace with id 0, like the editor does for an empty one.

### Slice 1: workspace save (`SaveWorkspace { id, base, workspace }`, `WorkspaceSaved { id, result }`)

Tests: `save_workspace_stores_the_payload`, `failed_save_keeps_the_input`, `new_workspace_id_comes_from_the_backend`. The test backend records the effect and answers with a snapshot whose saved record has a working directory the editor never held (`/workspace/backend-canonical`), so a screen that writes its pending copy is told from one that applies the snapshot.

Red: `900e06f` plus the type scaffold. Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `3 tests run: 0 passed, 3 failed`.

```text
save_workspace_stores_the_payload      roster [("x", "/workspace/payments-platform"), ...] has no ("x", "/workspace/backend-canonical")
failed_save_keeps_the_input            save dialog Error row: `write failed: ~/.jackin/workspaces is not writable…`, not `disk full`
new_workspace_id_comes_from_the_backend  left: 100   right: 42   (the id is the backend's)
```

Green: `3 tests run: 3 passed`, and the whole `jackin-console` suite with `--all-features` passes with the 59 `functional` journeys and their baselines unchanged.

### Slice 2: global settings and accounts (`SaveGlobal { base, global }`, `SaveAccounts { base, registry }`)

Tests: `save_global_stores_the_payload`, `trust_rows_reach_the_roles_only_through_the_snapshot`, `failed_global_save_keeps_the_input`, `save_accounts_stores_the_payload`, `failed_accounts_save_keeps_the_form`. T38 names `save_global_and_accounts_store_payloads`; it is split into five tests so that a red global half does not hide the accounts half. The trust test gives the role a source written with a scheme (`https://github.com/acme-labs/roles-experimental`): the old loop in `settings.rs` skipped every role whose source label starts with the trust row's source, so on the demo roles it never changed anything, and only a source that contains the row's source without starting with it reaches the `r.trusted = t.trusted` line. The sim therefore returns the roles it had, which is what the demo showed.

Red: the commit `4f19006` plus the type scaffold (`Effect::SaveGlobal` and `Effect::SaveAccounts` with their payloads, `Msg::GlobalSaved { result }`, `Msg::AccountsSaved { result }`; the settings screen sends the payload and still writes the world itself, the accounts screen is unchanged). Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `8 tests run: 3 passed, 5 failed` (the 3 passes are slice 1).

```text
save_global_stores_the_payload                           the world must be the reply snapshot, not the pending copy   (dco_signoff is true: the pending copy)
trust_rows_reach_the_roles_only_through_the_snapshot     left: []   right: ["data-eng"]   (the screen trusted the role)
failed_global_save_keeps_the_input                       Error row: `write failed: ~/.jackin/config.toml is not writable…`, not `disk full`
save_accounts_stores_the_payload                         one SaveAccounts expected, got []
failed_accounts_save_keeps_the_form                      the form is gone and the status reads `Saved Claude · Spare`; World.accounts holds Spare
```

Decisions: the accounts screen builds the new registry on a clone and sends `SaveAccounts` for the four changes it makes (save form, enable or disable, set default, remove); it keeps what to do on success (`Saving`: status, selection, unfold, close the form) and does it when the reply arrives. A failed reply keeps the form open with the reply text in its error line, or posts the text as an error when no form is open. The sim removes a registered account from every workspace that named it, and leaves the references of a discovered twin that the registry drops, as before.

Green: `8 tests run: 8 passed`; the whole `jackin-console` suite with `--all-features` passes (216 tests, the 59 journeys and their baselines unchanged).

### Slice 3: delete a workspace and restore an instance (`DeleteWorkspace { id }`, `Restore { instance }`)

Tests: `delete_workspace_is_an_effect`, `refused_delete_leaves_the_row`, `restore_is_an_effect`, `refused_restore_stays_on_the_row`. T38 names `delete_workspace_is_an_effect` and `restore_is_an_effect` with an ok half and an error half; the error halves are separate tests. The delete test answers with a snapshot that removes the workspace and keeps the instances' link to it (the sim and the old screen unlink them), and the restore test answers with `last_seen_secs = 12345` and no daemon, so a screen that writes the roster itself is told from one that applies the snapshot. The restore test does not tick: the reply has no delay, and a tick in the attached capsule bumps `last_seen_secs` again (`capsule.rs`, the per-tick heartbeat, which this step leaves alone).

Red: the commit `591814f` plus the type scaffold (`Effect::DeleteWorkspace`, `Effect::Restore`, `Msg::WorkspaceDeleted`, `Msg::Restored`, a sim arm for each; the manager still deletes and restores by itself). Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `12 tests run: 8 passed, 4 failed`.

```text
delete_workspace_is_an_effect          left: []   right: [DeleteWorkspace { id: 1 }]
refused_delete_leaves_the_row          left: [2, 3, 4]   right: [1, 2, 3, 4]   (the workspace ids; the row was removed anyway)
restore_is_an_effect                   left: []   right: [Restore { instance: "jk-c41e" }]
refused_restore_stays_on_the_row       left: Running   right: PreservedDirty
```

Decisions: the manager sends the effect and does the rest when the reply arrives (`Msg::WorkspaceDeleted`, `Msg::Restored`): apply the snapshot, then the status, the selection and, for a restore, the attach. The daemon of a restored instance is still created by the manager (`attach_restored`), the same four lines as before; design 6.4 moves them to `Go::Attach` with B8a (`Backend::initial_panes`), so that step edits `attach_restored`. The sim sets `Running` and the restore time on the instance it restores and unlinks the instances of a deleted workspace, as the screens did.

Green: `12 tests run: 12 passed`; the whole suite passes (220 tests, the `jackin_launch_reconnect` journey and the other baselines unchanged).

### Slice 4: role load and trust (`TrustRole { role }`, `RoleTrusted`, `RoleLoaded { role, result }`)

Tests: `role_load_comes_from_the_snapshot`, `failed_role_load_names_the_reply_error`, `trust_dialog_sends_trust_role`, `refused_trust_leaves_the_role_untrusted`. T38 has one test, `role_load_and_trust_are_effects`; it is split in four. The trust test answers `TrustRole` with a snapshot that trusts the role and leaves the global trust rows alone, so a screen that finds a row itself is told from one that applies the snapshot. `failed_role_load_names_the_reply_error` guards the one write that moved into `World::absorb` (`load_error` of a failed load); it is green on the scaffold by design, because the old screen also showed the reply error and set `load_error`, so it can only go red by breaking the move (named break: delete the `RoleLoaded` arm of `absorb`).

Red: the commit `189e8e4` plus the type scaffold (`Effect::TrustRole`, `Msg::RoleTrusted`, `Msg::RoleLoaded { role, result }`, the editor reading `result.is_ok()`). Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `16 tests run: 13 passed, 3 failed`.

```text
role_load_comes_from_the_snapshot        left: "Docs and release notes"   right: "loaded by the backend"
trust_dialog_sends_trust_role            left: [LoadRole { role: "data-eng" }]   right: [TrustRole { role: "data-eng" }, LoadRole { role: "data-eng" }]
refused_trust_leaves_the_role_untrusted  left: [LoadRole { role: "data-eng" }]   right: [TrustRole { role: "data-eng" }]
```

Decisions: accepting the trust dialog sends `TrustRole`; the editor starts the load when `RoleTrusted` carries a snapshot, and a refused trust ends the load with the reply text as an error. The editor applies the snapshot of a load and no longer touches `trusted`, `in_registry` or `load_error`; a failed load leaves `load_error` to `World::absorb`, the same place that takes the doctor report in. The demo literal `roles-experimental` is gone from the screens: the sim trusts the row whose `source` equals the role's repository or path.

Green: `16 tests run: 16 passed`; the whole suite passes with the baselines unchanged. `PENDING` loses the `roles-experimental` row.

### Slice 5: the mount form keeps its error (`keep_open_on_save`, the `save` action)

Tests: `mount_form_keeps_its_error`, `mount_form_saves_a_valid_mount_once`. The first types a relative destination (`relative`) and clicks Save; the second fills a Git source and an absolute destination and clicks Save. The second is the T38 negative case ("a valid submit closes the form once and adds the mount"); it passes on the old code by design, as a guard for the new route, and goes red if the success path stops closing the form or closes the editor with it (one `cx.close()` too many).

Red: the commit `2659664`, no scaffold needed (no new type). Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects mount_form`, exit 100, `2 tests run: 1 passed, 1 failed`.

```text
mount_form_keeps_its_error     the form closed:   (the screen is the editor's Mounts tab with the two old mounts; no error line, nothing typed)
```

Decisions: the mount form is built with `.keep_open_on_save()` like the env form, and `ConfigTabs::on_modal` routes `FormAction("save")` of `cfg.mount` to `save_mount`. The `ModalResult::Form(Some(values))` arm of `cfg.mount` is deleted: a form that stays open on save never produces it.

Green: `2 tests run: 2 passed`; the whole suite passes (226 tests, baselines unchanged).

### Slice 6: refresh, validation and the replies that carry failure (`Prewarmed`, `Stopped`, `Purged`, `AccountRefreshed { result }`, `AccountValidated { result }`)

Tests: `refresh_result_comes_from_the_backend`, `the_sim_refreshes_as_it_did`, `the_sim_keeps_the_last_good_values_when_the_broker_is_down`, `a_failed_stop_shows_the_reply_error`, `a_refused_purge_reports_its_reason`, `a_failed_prewarm_shows_the_reply_error`, `a_failed_validation_shows_the_reply_error`. T38 has `refresh_result_comes_from_the_backend` and `replies_carry_failure`; the second is split in four. The two sim tests are guards: they state today's outcome of a refresh (the first meter of Personal goes from 38% to 39%, `Refreshed Claude · Personal · current · 4 windows`; with the broker down the values stay, the account goes `Stale` and the status reads `broker unreachable · last good kept`), were read from the code before the move, and pass before and after. The failure tests deliver the reply with `App::deliver`, as `tests/external.rs` does. `tests/external.rs` and `tests/vocabulary.rs` built `Msg::Stopped` and `Msg::Purged` by hand; they now build the reply the way a backend does, a snapshot in which the instance has left.

Red: the commit `baf664e` plus the type scaffold (`Refresh`, `Validation`, the new `Msg` shapes; the screens ignore the results). Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `24 tests run: 19 passed, 5 failed`.

```text
refresh_result_comes_from_the_backend    left: Some(39)   right: Some(40)          (the screen added its own point)
a_failed_stop_shows_the_reply_error      left: "Instance 7f3a stopped"   right: "docker is not answering"
a_failed_prewarm_shows_the_reply_error   left: "Prewarmed payments-platform · derived image cached"   right: "the image build failed"
a_refused_purge_reports_its_reason       left: "Purged c41e · container, sidecar, volume and recovery state removed"   right: "purge refused: 2 unpushed commits would be lost"
a_failed_validation_shows_the_reply_error  status: "Claude · Personal · Source validated · quota readable"   (the screen validated by itself)
```

Decisions: the sim computes a refresh in `sim::provider::refresh` (the code that was `apply_refresh` in the accounts screen, with `+1`, the locked 1Password, the rate limit and the broker outage) and a validation in `Backend::settle`, both from the world when the reply falls due. `World::absorb` writes the reply into the account (`usage`, `issue`, `last_refresh_secs`, or the validation state), so no screen writes it; the accounts screen only shows the status. A failed refresh marks the account stale or failed with the reply text as its issue, and a failed validation marks it invalid with the reply text. `Stopped` and `Purged` carry a snapshot; the manager applies it, drops the daemon of the instance and shows the status, or shows the reply error and clears the busy row.

Deviation (visible only on the Usage screen, no baseline or test covers it): before, a refresh answered while the Usage screen was current ran a shorter rule there (freshness from the old issue code only: no `+1`, no locked-1Password failure, no broker outage). Both screens now take the one outcome the backend computed, so a refresh started on the Usage screen reads one point higher on the first meter, as it already did on the Accounts screen.

Green: `25 tests run: 25 passed`; the whole suite passes (232 tests, baselines unchanged). `PENDING` loses the `refresh_fails` row of `screens/accounts.rs`.

### Slice 7: the simulator owns `last_seen`, the first refresh failure, the second client, and the working-directory listing (`Backend::due`, `Refreshed { result }`, `Backend::file_source` in the editor)

Tests: `a_backend_owns_last_seen`, `a_backend_opens_no_takeover`, `the_sim_fails_the_first_refresh_then_bumps_last_seen`, `the_sim_opens_the_second_client_at_45_seconds`, `the_working_directory_picker_lists_the_source`, `the_working_directory_picker_lists_what_the_demo_listed`. T38 names `sim_owns_last_seen_and_takeover`: with a test backend 60 s of ticks leave `Instance.last_seen_secs` unchanged and open no takeover; with `SimBackend` both still happen. It is four tests (manager and capsule, test backend and sim). The two `the_sim_*` tests and `the_working_directory_picker_lists_what_the_demo_listed` are guards that pass before and after: the hard-cases world's first refresh fails and bumps nothing, the next ones bump the live instances, and the second client `tty004 · MacBook` attaches after 45 s of world time. The picker guard states what the demo listed: `6 items`, `adr`, `3 items` in the order of the demo files and only direct children.

Red: the commit `ea87c2d` plus the type scaffold (`Msg::Refreshed { result }`; the world still holds `refresh_fails` and `takeover_at_ms` and the manager still bumps `last_seen_secs`). The editor is the old one. Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `31 tests run: 28 passed, 3 failed`.

```text
a_backend_owns_last_seen                 left (after 60 s): [("jk-7f3a", 1788401697), ...]   right (before): [("jk-7f3a", 1788401637), ...]   (the manager bumped it)
a_backend_opens_no_takeover              the status line read `tty004 · MacBook attached to this instance` 63 times in 60 s with a backend that raises nothing
the_working_directory_picker_lists_the_source   the picker lists the two mounts only (`/workspace/payments-platfo…`, `/workspace/libs`); the sub-directories the source returns are missing, because the old editor read the emptied `World.fs`
```

Decisions: the manager asks `Effect::Refresh` every 5 s and applies the reply; `SimBackend` settles it by failing the first one in the hard-cases world (`instance index unreadable`, shown as `Refresh failed: … · showing last-good rows`) and by raising `Instance.last_seen_secs` of the live instances afterwards. `World::absorb` marks the daemon stale when a refresh fails. `Backend::due` is called on every tick; the sim answers it with the second client once its time has come and only when an instance is running, as `World::tick` did. `refresh_fails` (the broker outage) is a field of the sim, set by `SimBackend::hard_cases()`; `World.refresh_fails`, `World.takeover_at_ms` and `world::Takeover` are gone. `PENDING` is left with the B7a and B8a rows.

The editor's working-directory picker lists the sub-directories of a host mount through `Backend::file_source(w).list(source)` and writes each entry's `meta` as the row detail. The rows, their order and their texts are the ones the old code gave for every mount of every fixture world (the guard above compares the demo rows; a scan of the fixtures found no direct child of a mounted directory that is a repository, `git: Some(_)`). One corner differs: a repository that is a direct child of a mounted directory now reads `git · main` (what the file browser shows for it) where it read `git`, because the default source folds the branch into `meta` and the picker used the raw `meta`. A demo user reaches it only by mounting a parent such as `~/src`.

Green: `31 tests run: 31 passed`; the whole suite passes (239 tests, baselines unchanged).

## B8a: PaneBody, ViewportOps and the script player in sim (BX24, G19)

Plan: the approved P9.3 design (v7.1) section 6.4, 6.7 and the B8a row of 7.2, and test plan (v7.1) `script_bodies_unchanged` with the review amendment N11 (each case in a named test, each path naming its crate). Tests: `tests/viewport_ops.rs` (root crate, `-p junie-tui`), `apps/jackin/tests/pane_body.rs` (both shapes), `apps/jackin/tests/script_pane.rs` (feature `demo`), and the edited `apps/jackin/tests/library_has_no_demo.rs` (`-p jackin-console`). The tests were written before the code and run against 7424497 first.

Reproduce one record:

```sh
cargo nextest run --locked --no-fail-fast -p <crate> --all-features --test <file> -E 'test(=<name>)'
```

### Red on 7424497

None of the new types exists on 7424497, so three files fail to compile; the fourth fails an assertion.

- `tests/viewport_ops.rs` (exit 101): `error[E0432]: unresolved import junie_tui::widgets::viewport_ops`.
- `apps/jackin/tests/pane_body.rs` (exit 101): `error[E0432]: unresolved imports jackin_console::panes::PaneBody, PaneKey, PaneKind, PaneKindTag, PaneNote, PaneRequest, PaneSpawn, PasteOutcome`; `error[E0407]: method spawn_pane is not a member of trait Backend`; `error[E0407]: method initial_panes is not a member of trait Backend`; `error[E0046]: not all trait items implemented, missing: session_script`.
- `apps/jackin/tests/script_pane.rs` (exit 101): `error[E0432]: unresolved imports jackin_console::panes::PaneBody, PaneKey, PaneKindTag, PaneRequest, PaneSpawn, PasteOutcome`.
- `apps/jackin/tests/library_has_no_demo.rs`, with the three B8a rows of `PENDING` deleted and the `ScriptPane` rule added (exit 100): `real_tree_is_clean` fails with

```text
thread 'real_tree_is_clean' panicked at apps/jackin/tests/library_has_no_demo.rs:464:5:
backend.rs:16 names demo item `SessionScript`
backend.rs:343 names demo item `SessionScript`
panes.rs:41 names demo item `AgentProcess`
panes.rs:36 names demo item `SessionScript`
Summary 12 tests run: 11 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped
```

`the_script_player_lives_only_in_sim` passes on 7424497 because it scans synthetic trees; its proof is the named break below. The deleted `PENDING` rows (`SessionScript` in `backend.rs` and `panes.rs`, `AgentProcess` in `panes.rs`) are the whole B8a share of the list. `Step` had no row and has no rule: `screens/prelude.rs` has its own `Step` enum.

A compile error shows only that the API is missing. The named breaks below show that each test fails on the implemented code when one behaviour is wrong.

### The characterization trace

`script_bodies_unchanged` rests on the approved baselines, and they cover little of the Capsule. A throwaway test (not committed) drove the Capsule through three scenarios: 280 frames of `capsule-multi` (typing, paste, the scripted replies, tabs, splits, zoom, resize, wheel and page keys on the 1,973-line pane, drag selection, copy, the pane menu, export, the palette, clear, close dialogs, resize to four sizes), `returning` launch to Capsule and reconnect, and a restore from the manager. After every input it recorded each cell's symbol, colour and modifiers, the hardware cursor, the status line and the clipboard output. The run on 7424497 and the runs after each B8a commit are identical (`diff -r` prints nothing). Changing one key arm in `ScriptPane::key` made the trace differ, so it can fail.

### Named breaks

#### `the_ops_scroll_exactly_like_the_text_viewport` (viewport_ops): paging down to the end no longer returns to the live tail

Break in `src/widgets/viewport_ops.rs`:

```diff
--- src/widgets/viewport_ops.rs
+++ src/widgets/viewport_ops.rs
@@ -98,5 +98,4 @@
     fn page_down(&mut self) {
         self.scroll.page_down();
-        self.follow = self.is_at_tail();
     }
```

Red (exit 100):

```text
thread 'the_ops_scroll_exactly_like_the_text_viewport' (346076152) panicked at tests/viewport_ops.rs:72:9:
assertion `left == right` failed
left: false
right: true
Summary [   0.009s] 1 test run: 0 passed, 1 failed, 4 skipped
```

Revert, then green (exit 0):

```text
Summary [   0.007s] 1 test run: 1 passed, 4 skipped
```

#### `draw_shows_the_hardware_cursor_only_when_the_caret_is_allowed_and_live` (viewport_ops): draw ignores the host's `caret_allowed`

Break in `src/widgets/viewport_ops.rs`:

```diff
--- src/widgets/viewport_ops.rs
+++ src/widgets/viewport_ops.rs
@@ -158,5 +158,5 @@
     ) -> Option<Position> {
         let mut view = self.clone();
-        view.caret_visible = self.caret_visible && caret_allowed;
+        view.caret_visible = self.caret_visible;
         view.render(area, buf, ctx, bg);
```

### Gate evidence outside the tests

- The product library holds no script player: `cargo build --locked -p jackin-console --no-default-features`, then `nm -C target/debug/libjackin_console.rlib | grep -c 'ScriptPane\|AgentProcess\|SessionScript\|jackin_console::sim'` prints 0, and the same `nm | grep -c jackin_console::panes` prints 886, so the grep sees the library.
- `git status --porcelain -- tests/functional/baselines apps/jackin/tests/functional/baselines` prints nothing after every commit.

## B7a: the cockpit fed by LaunchEvent, Ready(instance), SimLaunch in sim (BX25, G9, G10)

Plan: design v7.1 section 5.1 (rows "Launch ready" and "Retry credentials"), sections 3.7.3 and 4.1 (the launch plan) and section 7 (B7a), test plan v7.1 T39 with its review verdict (GO). Tests: `apps/jackin/tests/launch_feed.rs`, feature `demo`, 14 tests. Five are guards that state what the simulated launch leaves behind and pass before and after (`the_sim_launch_ends_as_it_did`, `the_sim_retry_unlocks_the_session_and_resumes`, `the_sim_cancel_records_a_failed_setup`, `the_sim_abort_records_a_failed_setup`, `the_sim_failure_records_a_failed_setup`); their literals were read from the code at `06b55e7` before the move (instance id, container, working directory, creation time, run id, repository, branch, accounts, the daemon label and its one pane with the launch account). Nine are the T39 tests. T39 names four: `ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance`, `failed_event_builds_from_the_event` and `retry_credentials` keep their names; `daemon_is_built_by_attach` is `the_cockpit_builds_no_daemon` (see the first deviation); the rest are the cancel and abort paths (`cancel_keeps_the_record_the_feed_returns`, `ctrl_c_keeps_the_record_the_feed_returns`, `a_cancel_without_a_record_adds_none`), the plain-text answer of the credential dialog (`plain_text_resumes_the_feed`) and the run id (`run_id_comes_from_the_feed`), because the cockpit built a record or flipped the session on each of those paths too. The test backend (`FeedBackend`) wraps the simulator for everything but `Backend::launch`, which returns a scripted `LaunchFeed` that records the calls it receives. Its instance has a container, a working directory (`/sandbox/project`), a default branch and no repository that the cockpit could not have made up, in a world whose workspace name matches a repository.

What changed:

- `launch.rs` keeps `LaunchTarget` and `LaunchEvent`, and gains `ReadyInstance { instance, run_id }`, `FailedLaunch { failure, instance }`, the trait `LaunchFeed` (`run_id`, `poll(&World)`, `retry_credentials(&mut dyn OnePassword)`, `use_plain_text`, `cancel(&World) -> Option<Instance>`) and `launch_accounts`, the one rule for the accounts a launch offers (the cockpit draws them, the simulator stores them in the record). `LaunchEvent::Failed` and `LaunchEvent::Ready` carry the records. `LaunchRun` and `LaunchPlan` are gone from the library.
- `Backend::launch(&World, &LaunchTarget) -> Box<dyn LaunchFeed>` replaces `Backend::launch_plan`. It is a required method, so a backend that cannot launch says so (the product-shape test backend is `unreachable!`, like its other unused methods).
- `sim::launch` holds `LaunchPlan` and `SimLaunch` (the old `LaunchRun` with the record, the container name, the run id, the instance id counter and the `1Password locked` sentence). `SimBackend::launch_plan` is an inherent method; `Backend::launch` builds a `SimLaunch` from it. `World::new_instance_id` moved into `sim::launch` as a private function.
- `CockpitScreen` holds a `Box<dyn LaunchFeed>`, the `LaunchContent` and a small `Progress` (stage states, failure, done, cancelled) built from the events. It adds the records with the new `World::admit` (push and `sync_arbiter`, the two lines it wrote five times), takes the run id from the feed, answers the credential dialog through the feed, and creates no daemon. `instance_id` is an `Option`: it is set from the record of the failure or of the cancel.
- `Go::Attach` opens the first session of an instance that arrives from the cockpit without a daemon (`App::open_first_session`).

Deviations from the design, each small:

1. Daemon creation. Design 6.4 and the T39 row `daemon_is_built_by_attach` build the daemon in `Go::Attach` through `Backend::initial_panes`. That method belongs to B8a and is not on this branch, so B7a builds it with the calls the cockpit made (`Daemon::new(label, session_script)`, `new_tab(Some(agent), account, now, false)`), moved to `App::open_first_session`. The attach does it only when it comes from the cockpit and no daemon exists. A general rule ("Running and no daemon") would change the hard-cases instance `jk-e0e0` (running, deliberately without a daemon, `No sessions` in the capsule), which no baseline covers. The account comes from the cockpit screen because `Instance` does not carry the account a launch used and the capsule shows it (`Claude Code (Work)`); `initial_panes(&Instance)` has no source for it either. The discriminating test is `the_cockpit_builds_no_daemon`: a `Ready` for an instance that is not running is refused by the attach, and no daemon exists afterwards. The old cockpit created it first.
2. The label of that daemon is `World::workspace_label(instance)` (B5b). For a launch in a directory without a workspace the cockpit used the last word of its own target label with `~/` trimmed (`code/foo`), where the label is now `foo`. It shows only as the prompt of a shell tab opened later in such a capsule (inferred from the code; no test or baseline launches in a directory).
3. `ReadyInstance.run_id` is in the design and in T39, and the cockpit does not read it: the run id is shown from the first frame, so it comes from `LaunchFeed::run_id`. The field is for a backend that learns its run late.
4. `retry_credentials` takes the console's `OnePassword`, because the simulated launch must keep unlocking the session the accounts screen reads afterwards (`the_sim_retry_unlocks_the_session_and_resumes`). A backend that unlocks elsewhere ignores it. The design says "the sim flips its own session"; the session is a `World` field, not the simulator's.
5. `LaunchFeed::poll` and `cancel` take `&World`. The records carry the time, the workspace name and the repository as they are when the event happens, the way the cockpit read them.

Red: the commit `829f881` (the guards) plus a type scaffold in a scratch worktree, never committed: the new `LaunchEvent`, `ReadyInstance`, `FailedLaunch`, `LaunchFeed`, a provided `Backend::launch`, with the old cockpit kept on `LaunchRun` and the old event enum renamed. Without it the tests do not compile, and a compile error is not a disagreement. It ran in its own target directory (a shared one reused the main worktree's binaries and passed the tests; that run is discarded). Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test launch_feed --test library_has_no_demo --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `25 tests run: 15 passed, 10 failed`. The five guards and the other ten `library_has_no_demo` tests pass; the nine new tests and `real_tree_is_clean` fail:

```text
ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance   no instance `jk-5b0x` in the roster (the cockpit ran its own launch and ignored the feed)
the_cockpit_builds_no_daemon               left: ""   right: "Cannot attach: 5b0x is not running"
failed_event_builds_from_the_event         never reached `The sandbox could not be created`
run_id_comes_from_the_feed                 the Debug info reads `Run id  run-202609030914-b5df`, not `R-7`
retry_credentials                          never reached `Credential source unavailable`
plain_text_resumes_the_feed                never reached `Credential source unavailable`
cancel_keeps_the_record_the_feed_returns   left: 0   right: 1   (cancels the feed received)
ctrl_c_keeps_the_record_the_feed_returns   left: 0   right: 1
a_cancel_without_a_record_adds_none        left: 0   right: 1
real_tree_is_clean                         backend.rs:15,415,416 and launch.rs:22,47,68,127-129,169,185,186 name demo item `LaunchPlan`;
                                           launch.rs:174 names `1Password locked: unlock the app and retry`
```

These reds are mostly "the cockpit does not consult the feed", which is what the step changes. The named breaks below show that each test also catches the fabrication it was written for. Each break was applied to the finished code, `launch_feed` was run, and the file was restored (`git checkout`):

| Break | Red |
|---|---|
| the cockpit sets `instance.container = jackin-<role>` on `Ready` | `ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance`, `the_sim_launch_ends_as_it_did` |
| the cockpit calls `w.op.set_session(SignedIn)` on Retry | `retry_credentials` |
| the cockpit fills `instance.github` by the name-suffix match on `Ready` | `ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance` |
| the cockpit creates the daemon on `Ready` | `the_cockpit_builds_no_daemon`, `ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance`, `the_sim_launch_ends_as_it_did` |
| the cockpit adds a made-up record when `cancel` returns `None` | `a_cancel_without_a_record_adds_none` |

Green: `14 tests run: 14 passed`; the whole `jackin-console` suite with `--all-features` passes (253 tests, the 59 `functional` journeys and every baseline unchanged), and so does `jackin_launch_*`. `PENDING` loses the three B7a rows (`LaunchPlan` in `backend.rs` and `launch.rs`, the `1Password locked` sentence in `launch.rs`); it keeps the B8a rows (`SessionScript` in `backend.rs` and `panes.rs`, `AgentProcess` in `panes.rs`), which B8a removes.

For the B8a merge. B7a touches these lines that B8a also changes: the `Ready` arm of `screens/cockpit.rs` (the daemon creation is deleted, B8a's `Daemon::new(&wsname)`, `PaneRequest::new` and `spawn_pane` lines there go with it), and the `Go::Attach` arm and the new `App::open_first_session` in `app.rs` (the place where the old `Daemon::new(label, session_script)` and `new_tab(Some(agent), account, now, false)` calls now live, and where B8a's `PaneRequest` and `spawn_pane` calls belong). The `launch_plan` forwarding in `tests/effects.rs` and `tests/sources.rs` becomes `launch`, and `tests/product_mode.rs` gains an `unreachable!` `launch` next to its `session_script`; B8a moves that backend into `tests/product_support`. `library_has_no_demo.rs` loses only the B7a rows.

### B7a merge

B7a was merged into the B5b + B6 + B8a head (`1fd4d17`). B7a's temporary daemon path in `App::open_first_session` (called from `Go::Attach` when the attach comes from the cockpit and no daemon exists) is now the B8a form: `Daemon::new(&label)`, then for each request of `w.backend.initial_panes(instance)` one `new_tab(&request, now, |spawn| backend.spawn_pane(&request, spawn))`. The B7a rule is kept: the daemon is built only for an attach from the cockpit with no daemon. The cockpit `Ready` arm keeps B7a's version, so B8a's daemon lines there are gone. `launch`, `spawn_pane` and `initial_panes` sit side by side in `Backend` and in the forwarding backends of `tests/effects.rs`, `tests/sources.rs`, `tests/product_mode.rs` and `tests/launch_feed.rs`; no `session_script` or `launch_plan` is left. `PENDING` in `tests/library_has_no_demo.rs` is empty.

One difference from the plain B8a form. The simulator's `initial_panes` has no account (an `Instance` carries none), and the guard `the_sim_launch_ends_as_it_did` (and `ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance`) went red with `left: None, right: Some("acct-claude-work")` when the attach used its requests as they came. The capsule shows the account (`Claude Code (Work)`), so the attach gives an agent request that has no account the account of the cockpit's launch. Both tests are green again.

`daemon_is_built_by_attach` (T39) is restored next to `the_cockpit_builds_no_daemon`, which stays. The test backend answers `initial_panes` with the simulator's agent pane and a shell pane, and records the instance ids it was asked for. The test waits for the capsule and asserts that the backend was asked once, for `jk-5b0x`, and that the daemon has two tabs: the Claude Code pane with the launch account, then the shell.

## Batch fix round (B5b, B6, B8a, B7a)

Review: `P9.3-B5b-B6-B8a-review` (MEDIUM 1, MEDIUM 2) and the account deviation in "B7a merge". Base `fda8afb` (the B7a merge on `1fd4d17`). Three commits on `wt/P9.3-int`: `c884df7` (settle), `0593159` (roles admin), `e0ea668` (account). No baseline moved: `git status --porcelain -- tests/functional/baselines apps/jackin/tests/functional/baselines` is empty after the full run.

### MEDIUM 1: one due reply is settled and applied at a time

`World::take_due` drained every due job and called `Backend::settle` for each before any reply was dispatched, so the second snapshot was computed from the world that did not yet hold the first reply and put it back. Now `take_due` pops the earliest due job, settles it and returns `Option<Msg>`; a job whose `settle` answers `None` is dropped and the next due job is tried. `deliver_due` is `while let Some(m) = take_due() { dispatch }`. `World::tick` takes at most one job and leaves the rest to the `deliver_due` that follows every tick (`handle_input` and `on_tick`), in the same order as before (the sort is stable, a reply started by a dispatch queues behind the jobs that were already due).

Test: `tests/effects.rs` `two_stops_due_in_one_tick_both_land`. `Effect::Stop` for `jk-7f3a` and `jk-9b02` (both Running in `Returning`), then 40 ticks; both must leave Running and lose their daemon. Red on the unfixed code (the test compiles against it, so this is a plain red on `fda8afb` plus the test), `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects -E 'test(two_stops)'`, exit 100, `1 test run: 0 passed, 1 failed`:

```text
two_stops_due_in_one_tick_both_land   assertion `left != right` failed: the second reply must not put jk-7f3a back
                                        left: Running   right: Running
```

Green: the same command, `1 passed`. `tests/snapshot.rs` `the_sim_never_hands_out_a_workspace_id_in_use` reads the new `Option<Msg>`.

### MEDIUM 2: the roles admin screens apply the snapshot

| Action | Effect (new fields) | Reply | World change |
|---|---|---|---|
| bind | `BindRole { role, agent, base, account }`, `base` is the binding the screen saw | `RoleBound { role, agent, result: Result<Box<Snapshot>, String> }` | `finish_bind` applies the snapshot |
| add | `AddRole { location }` | `RoleAdded { location, result: Result<(RoleName, Box<Snapshot>), String> }` | `finish_add` applies it, selects the name from the reply |
| check | `CheckRole { role, doc }` | `RoleChecked { role, result: Result<Box<Snapshot>, String> }` | the roles screen applies it |
| remove | new `RemoveRole { role }` (there was no effect) | new `RoleRemoved { role, result }` | the roles screen applies it |
| save (editor) | `SaveRole { role, base, doc }`, `base` is the opened manifest | `RoleSaved { role, result }` | `finish_save` applies it |

The sim settles all five from the world as it is when they fall due (`with_role` edits a copy of `snapshot(world)`): check 600 ms, save 900, bind 900, add 700, remove 0. The values are the old ones, so the demo shows the same thing: the same text, the same timings, and `bind_fails_once` still fails the first bind with the same sentence (it is now consumed at settle, not at `perform`). `Msg::job()` is `None` for `RoleRemoved`: the old removal showed no progress and the reply is immediate.

Two consequences of "no screen writes World after an effect":

- The `Running` marker of a check is no longer written into the record. Nothing could undo it when a check fails, so a failure reply would have left the world changed. The roles screen keeps it in its `checking` set and draws it from there (`check_state`); its list, detail, Check button and `animating` read the set. `RoleCheckState::Running` stays for the role editor, which keeps its own state.
- The role editor shares `CheckRole` with the roles screen and reads the findings from the reply snapshot without applying it (it checks an unsaved manifest). A failure reply now unlocks the editor (`check` back to `Unchecked`, status shows the reason). Before, a check could not fail.

Tests (`tests/effects.rs`, one success row and one failure row per action). Each success row uses a backend whose snapshot differs from what the screen would write (a second binding, a role named by the backend, a finding the sim never raises, a second role removed, a pinned model). Each failure row replies `Err` and asserts that `surfaces.roles` equals its value before the action, that the reason is shown, and that the input is kept (the add form keeps the location, the editor keeps the edit).

`role_bind_comes_from_the_snapshot`, `failed_role_bind_keeps_the_binding`, `role_add_comes_from_the_snapshot`, `failed_role_add_keeps_the_location`, `role_check_comes_from_the_snapshot`, `failed_role_check_leaves_the_record`, `role_remove_comes_from_the_snapshot`, `failed_role_remove_keeps_the_role`, `role_save_comes_from_the_snapshot`, `failed_role_save_keeps_the_edits`, and `failed_editor_check_unlocks_the_editor` for the editor path above.

Red. The reply types change shape, so the new tests do not compile against `fda8afb` and a compile error is not a disagreement. The red is a named break on the finished code, applied to the screen, run, and restored (`diff` against a copy shows no residue). A "write" break puts the old screen write back in place of `w.apply(snapshot)`; an "optimistic" break writes the pending copy before it looks at the reply, which is what the old screens' `ok` path did to the world:

```text
break                                   test                                       red
bind: write the binding itself          role_bind_comes_from_the_snapshot          the role is the reply snapshot's, not the screen's   left: None   right: Some("acct-claude-work")
bind: write before the reply            failed_role_bind_keeps_the_binding         assertion `left == right` failed (surfaces.roles differs from before)
add: push a record the screen builds    role_add_comes_from_the_snapshot           ["sentinel", "the-architect", "reviewer", "data-eng", "writer", "new-role"]
add: push before the reply              failed_role_add_keeps_the_location         assertion `left == right` failed (surfaces.roles differs from before)
check: write the findings itself        role_check_comes_from_the_snapshot         the role is the reply snapshot's, not the screen's   left: None   right: Some("acct-claude-work")
check: set Running before the reply     failed_role_check_leaves_the_record        assertion `left == right` failed (the record stays Running)
remove: retain the record itself        role_remove_comes_from_the_snapshot        the roster is the reply snapshot's, not the screen's   left: ["sentinel", "the-architect", "reviewer", "data-eng"]   right: ["sentinel", "the-architect", "reviewer"]
remove: retain before the reply         failed_role_remove_keeps_the_role          assertion `left == right` failed (surfaces.roles differs from before)
save: write the pending manifest        role_save_comes_from_the_snapshot          the manifest is the reply snapshot's, not the screen's   left: Ok(Some("pin"))   right: Ok(Some("backend-pinned"))
save: write before the reply            failed_role_save_keeps_the_edits           assertion `left == right` failed (surfaces.roles differs from before)
editor: keep Running after a failure    failed_editor_check_unlocks_the_editor     the screen still shows `checking` and the editor stays locked
```

Each break ran with `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test effects -E 'test(/<test>/)'`, exit 100, `1 failed` of the two rows (`role_bind`, `role_add`, `role_check`, `role_remove`, `role_save` filters). The first "check: set Running" attempt wrote `Done(vec![])`, which equals sentinel's own value and passed (exit 0); it proved nothing and is replaced by the `Running` break above. Green: `42 tests run` before the editor test, `43 tests run: 43 passed` after, and the whole `jackin-console` suite, 280 tests with every functional baseline unchanged.

### Account on `Instance`

`Instance.account: Option<AccountId>` (the type the domain uses) is the account a sandbox was launched with, the `jackin.dev/account` label of a real one. `Instance.accounts` stays the list the launch offered.

- `SimLaunch` keeps `account` from the launch target and writes it on every record; `use_plain_text` clears it, as the cockpit cleared its own copy.
- Fixtures: `jk-7f3a` has `acct-claude-work` and `jk-9b02` has `acct-codex-experiments`, the accounts of their fixture daemons. The other instances carry `None`; no screen reads the field.
- `SimBackend::initial_panes` puts `instance.account` on the agent `PaneRequest`.
- `App::open_first_session(instance)` loses its `account` parameter and the loop that filled an empty agent account from the cockpit. The rule "build the daemon only for an attach from the cockpit with no daemon" is unchanged (`router.cockpit_mut().is_some()`).
- `instance_fixed` lists `account: _` next to `github: _`; the probe instance in `tests/fixed_fields.rs` has `account: Some("probe-account")`. `Instance` is built in `tests/launch_feed.rs` (`launched` gets `Some(WORK)`) and `tests/pane_body.rs` (`None`).

Tests, in `tests/launch_feed.rs`: `the_agent_pane_runs_with_the_account_of_the_record` (record says `acct-codex-primary`, cockpit launched with `acct-claude-work`) and `a_record_without_an_account_gets_a_pane_without_one`; and `the_sim_launch_ends_as_it_did` gains `record.account == Some(WORK)`. Red: a scaffold in the working tree that keeps `Instance.account` but puts the old `app.rs` fill-in, the old `initial_panes` and a sim record with `account: None` back (never committed, restored afterwards). `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test launch_feed --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `17 tests run: 14 passed, 3 failed`:

```text
the_agent_pane_runs_with_the_account_of_the_record   the cockpit launched with acct-claude-work; the record says otherwise   left: Some("acct-claude-work")   right: Some("acct-codex-primary")
a_record_without_an_account_gets_a_pane_without_one  the cockpit must not fill in the account of its own launch   left: Some("acct-claude-work")   right: None
the_sim_launch_ends_as_it_did                        left: None   right: Some("acct-claude-work")
```

`ready_event_inserts_the_backend_instance` and `daemon_is_built_by_attach` pass on the scaffold and on the finished code. Green: `17 tests run: 17 passed`.

`tests/script_pane.rs` `the_sim_backend_proposes_the_instance_agent_as_the_first_pane` asserted a pane without an account for `jk-7f3a`. That is the old fill-in contract, and it went red as expected when the fixture got its account. It now asserts `acct-claude-work` and is named `..._agent_and_account_as_the_first_pane`.

### Deviations

- `RemoveRole` and `RoleRemoved` are new; the review named the removal as a screen write with no effect, and the B6 pattern needs one.
- `AddRole`, `CheckRole` and `RemoveRole` carry no `base`: they have nothing to compare against. `BindRole.base` and `SaveRole.base` are the previous binding and the opened manifest. The sim ignores both, as it ignores `base` in the B6 saves.
- The `Running` marker leaves the world (see above), and the role editor handles a failed check.
- `World::tick` settles one job; the others wait for the `deliver_due` after it. During the intro, outro and handoff routes `tick_step` returns before it dispatches the messages it took, so those routes now drop at most one reply where they dropped all of them. No test or baseline runs a reply through those routes.
- One test was renamed (`script_pane`, above).

compile on 1fd4d17. The red run below is therefore on 1fd4d17 plus only the data
commit (the new `Fixed` fields, `openshell::env_edit_block`, the `edit_blocked`
of the two host-variable accounts of `openshell-fixed`, `EditBlocked::reason`)
and the test files: the screen code is the code of 1fd4d17. The baselines of
the 13 new looks were already accepted in that state, so a test that reaches
its first `visual` call fails on the frozen check when the look is wrong.
`jackin_fixed_account_edit_refuses_a_host_variable_account` fails only there:
the menu border and the `e` key were already built by B3, and the Edit… button,
the click refusal and the footer hint are what the baseline pins.

## Batch fix round (B7a, B4c)

Reviews: `P9.3-B7a-B4c-review` (MEDIUM 1, LOW 2) and `P9.3-B4c-ux` (F1, F2). Base `68f441d`. Under D27 no approved baseline moves except the exact rows the UX findings re-accept. Every red below is a run on the unfixed library code of `68f441d` with the new test (or rule) in place; the fix then turned it green.

### MEDIUM 1: no invented telemetry line, typed key or stage time on the product path

The cockpit drew three things no backend gave it: the collector in the debug info (`otlp://collector.internal:4317`), the credentials line after "Enter plain text instead" (`masked("Q1zx")`, "typed once for this launch"), and a duration per finished stage (`stage.ticks * 0.033 * 3.0`). Now each comes from the feed, and the cockpit draws nothing when the feed gives nothing:

- `LaunchEvent::StageTimed { stage, elapsed_ms }` carries the time of a finished stage; the rail meta is set only by this event. `SimLaunch` sends it after `StageChanged(Done)` with `spec.ticks * 99` ms (the old factor, so the text is the same: `1.4 s`, `1.8 s`, ...).
- `LaunchFeed::telemetry() -> Option<&str>` (default `None`); the Telemetry row of the debug info exists only when it is `Some`. `SimLaunch` returns the old endpoint.
- `LaunchFeed::use_plain_text() -> Option<TypedKey>` (`origin`, `validation`); the credentials line changes only when the feed returns one. `SimLaunch` returns the old two lines, with the key built in `sim/launch.rs`.

Scanner rules added to `RULES` in `tests/library_has_no_demo.rs` (outside `sim/`, `fixtures/`, `bin/`): `literal("otlp://")`, `literal("Q1zx")`, `literal("typed once for this launch")`, `code("ticks as f64")`, `code("0 . 0 3 3")`. The `1Password locked: unlock the app and retry` rule is now `OnlyIn(["launch.rs", "screens/accounts.rs", "screens/cockpit.rs"])`.

Red 1, the rule tests against rules that do not exist yet (`cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test launch_feed --test library_has_no_demo --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `35 tests run: 29 passed, 6 failed`):

```text
library_has_no_demo the_cockpit_invents_no_telemetry_key_or_timing   otlp:// in screens/cockpit.rs: []   left: 0   right: 1
library_has_no_demo the_locked_sentence_is_scanned_in_the_cockpit    left: 0   right: 1
```

Red 2, the rules added, the cockpit not yet fixed (`--test library_has_no_demo`, exit 100, `14 tests run: 13 passed, 1 failed`): `real_tree_is_clean` names every occurrence in `68f441d`:

```text
screens/cockpit.rs:415 names demo item `otlp://`
screens/cockpit.rs:774 names demo item `Q1zx`
screens/cockpit.rs:776 names demo item `typed once for this launch`
screens/cockpit.rs:265 names demo item `ticks as f64`
screens/cockpit.rs:265 names demo item `0 . 0 3 3`
```

Red 3, the product-shape tests in `tests/launch_feed.rs` (same run as red 1; a `ScriptedFeed` that gives no endpoint, key line or time):

```text
a_feed_that_gives_no_timing_draws_none                              left: Some("1.4 s")   right: None
a_feed_that_names_no_collector_shows_no_telemetry                   the debug info holds `Telemetry  run R-7 -> otlp://collector.internal:4317`
a_feed_that_shows_no_key_leaves_the_credentials_line_alone          the line reads `plain text · masked API key · ••••••••…Q1zx` and `typed once for this launch · never stored`
```

Three positive tests (`a_timing_from_the_feed_is_drawn_for_its_stage`, `a_collector_from_the_feed_is_shown_in_the_debug_info`, `the_key_line_of_the_feed_is_what_the_credentials_line_shows`) use new API, so they do not compile on `68f441d`; they pin that the text shown is the feed's own (`40.0 s`, `otlp-test.invalid:1`, `typed by the operator`). Green: the whole `jackin-console` suite, `301 tests run: 301 passed`, and no functional baseline moved for this change (the demo is byte-identical).

### LOW 2: a reply that falls due on the intro, outro or handoff route is delivered

`World::tick` no longer calls `take_due`; `deliver_due` runs after every tick (`handle_input` and `on_tick`) on every route, and `tick` returns only `Backend::due`. The declared "drops at most one reply" deviation of the fix round is gone.

Test: `a_reply_due_on_the_handoff_is_delivered` (`Motion::Full`, scripted `Ready`, wait for `Route::Handoff`, `schedule(0, Msg::Refreshed)` with a one-workspace snapshot, one tick). Red on `68f441d`, same run as red 1:

```text
a_reply_due_on_the_handoff_is_delivered   the reply that fell due on the handoff was dropped   left: 4   right: 1
```

Green: `workspaces.len() == 1`. `two_stops_due_in_one_tick_both_land` stays green.

### UX F1: the host-variable Edit… reason names a way out that works

`env_edit_block` text is now `reads {var} · remove it, then add an account` (50 columns for `ZAI_API_KEY`, 56 for a name cut to 16 plus `…`). Tests changed: `the_edit_block_names_the_variable_and_cuts_a_long_name`, `a_blocked_edit_leaves_the_other_actions_open` (`tests/account_data.rs`) and `jackin_fixed_account_edit_refuses_a_host_variable_account` (`tests/functional/jackin.rs`).

Red (`--test account_data`, exit 100, `12 tests run: 11 passed, 1 failed`):

```text
the_edit_block_names_the_variable_and_cuts_a_long_name   left: "reads ZAI_API_KEY · change it with jackin account add"   right: "reads ZAI_API_KEY · remove it, then add an account"
```

### UX F2: a focused fixed menu item shows the cursor

`ContextMenu` marks a row `fixed` when the item is disabled and has a reason, so a focused one takes the live cursor fill; its label ink is `legible(text_muted, fill)`. An item with no reason, and an unfocused one, are unchanged. The muted `8a8a8a` is 1.94:1 on the fill (`2f5aa8`), so `legible` lifts it to `ffffff` (6.67:1); the label reads brighter than "muted" but clears the 3:1 target the review set. The shortcut cell of the row takes the same ink.

Test: `tests/fixed.rs` `a_focused_menu_item_with_a_reason_keeps_the_cursor_fill` (focused reasoned item has the fill and the lifted muted ink; unfocused reasoned item keeps `theme.disabled` and no fill; a disabled item without a reason keeps `theme.disabled` and no fill; a live item keeps its fill). Red on `68f441d` (`cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p junie-tui --test fixed -E 'test(/menu_item_with_a_reason/)'`, exit 100, the fix reverted with `git apply -R` and applied again afterwards):


## B5: pending validate, probe_folder, changes and the 1Password picker (BX4, G13)

Plan: design v7.1 sections 5.1 (row "Sync calls") and 7.2 (B5), test plan v7.1 section 5 ("B5, B7b: their baseline rows"). The v7.1 delta lists no B5 row by name, so the tests below are the B5 rows. Tests: `apps/jackin/tests/pending.rs` (feature `demo`, 22 tests) and `jackin_account_form_shows_a_pending_check_until_the_provider_answers` in `apps/jackin/tests/functional/jackin.rs`.

Decisions made while building:

- `Backend::validate`, `probe_folder` and `changes` are removed. Four effects replace them, each with a `Ticket` (from `World::next_ticket`) and a reply that carries it: `CheckSource` / `SourceChecked`, `ProbeFolder` / `FolderProbed`, `ReadChanges` / `ChangesRead`, `LoadOp` / `OpLoaded`. A reply whose ticket is not the one the screen waits for is ignored. A typed key travels as `PlainKey`, whose `Debug` prints `PlainKey(…)`.
- The 1Password picker (`OpFlow`) no longer calls `OnePassword::list_*` or runs a timer. It wants a listing (`take_request`), the app sends it in the same input step (`App::send_op_requests`), and the reply (`OpListing`: rows without values) fills the picker. `OnePassword::list_*` and `latency_ms` stay on the trait: the sim reads its data through them.
- The account form: Validate sends `CheckSource`; the form shows the three `… material / identity / quota   checking` rows, the Validate button busy (library `Button::busy`, no library change) and the status `Validating the source…`. Save sends the same check and, for a local folder, `ProbeFolder` after it; nothing is stored until the replies are in. Save during a Validate promotes the one check. An edit, the chooser buttons or Esc drop the pending check.
- The capsule's Inspect changes sends `ReadChanges` and shows `Reading changes…`; a second request while one is out is ignored.
- Sim timings: the picker 260 ms (unchanged), a typed key and a folder 0 ms (unchanged: `jackin_account_required_masked_save_cancel_and_remove` saves a typed key under a paused clock and failed when this was 900 ms), `ProbeFolder` and `ReadChanges` 0 ms. A 1Password reference takes 900 ms, the one new timing. No existing baseline or test reaches it.

### Red on 93949cf

None of the new types exists on 93949cf, so the scratch tree at 93949cf gets a type scaffold only: the four `Effect` and `Msg` variants, `OpScope`, `OpListing` and its rows, `PlainKey`, `Ticket`, the job keys, and default bodies for the three removed trait methods that call the sim. The screens and the sim behave as before (the sim ignores the new effects). The scaffold is never committed. Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test pending --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `22 tests run: 4 passed, 18 failed`.

```text

save_waits_for_the_check_and_then_saves                      assertion `left == right` failed   left: 0  (checks sent; the form checked by itself)
save_during_a_validate_saves_when_the_check_returns          one check serves both   left: 0
a_failed_check_shows_the_reply_error_in_the_form             the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_rejected_check_keeps_the_form_open_and_stores_nothing      the request was not sent; the backend saw [SaveAccounts { ... }]  (it saved without asking)
editing_the_form_drops_the_pending_check                     the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_reply_to_an_earlier_check_is_not_the_forms                 the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_reply_after_the_form_closed_does_nothing                   the request was not sent; the backend saw []
saving_a_folder_asks_the_backend_what_is_in_it               the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_folder_probe_that_fails_keeps_the_form_open                the request was not sent; the backend saw []
inspect_changes_waits_for_the_backend                        one ReadChanges expected, got []
an_empty_changes_reply_says_nothing_changed                  the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_failed_changes_read_shows_the_reply_error                  the request was not sent; the backend saw []
the_picker_loads_through_the_backend                         assertion `left == right` failed   left: []  (no LoadOp; the picker loaded on a timer)
choosing_an_account_asks_for_its_vaults                      the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_locked_listing_offers_a_retry_that_asks_again              the request was not sent; the backend saw []
a_listing_the_picker_has_left_behind_is_not_shown            the request was not sent; the backend saw []
the_sim_holds_a_1password_check_for_900_ms                   the form shows no `… material   checking` row (the check ended at once)
```

The four passes are guards that state today's outcome and must hold before and after: `the_sim_checks_a_typed_key_on_the_spot`, `the_sim_saves_a_folder_on_the_spot_with_the_kind_it_probes` (`~/.claude-work` saves as `ClaudeApiKeyEnv`), `the_sim_answers_a_changes_read_on_the_spot`, `the_sim_still_lists_the_picker_after_260_ms` (320 ms of ticks on 80 ms ticks, the same count on 93949cf and after).

The PTY journey on 93949cf (same scratch tree, the test and its baseline copied in), exit 100: `timed out: visible … material   checking` after 11.6 s, with the old synchronous result on screen.

### Named breaks

## B7b: the refusal view, the env prompt and the disabled picker rows (BX6, v6 5.2 items 15 and 16), and `x` on a blocked launch

Plan: design v7.1 section 7 (B7b), v6 section 5.2 (items 15 and 16, and the env prompt of BX6), v6 section 4.5 (the cockpit backstop texts) and test plan T9. Base `93949cf`. Under D27 no approved baseline moves; the three new looks are three added files. Tests: `apps/jackin/tests/launch_blocked.rs` (3), `launch_checks.rs` (9), five more in `launch_feed.rs`, one in `library_has_no_demo.rs`, a row in `entry.rs`, and four journeys in `apps/jackin/tests/functional/jackin.rs`.

What changed:

- `launch.rs` gains `Refusal { title, found, state, next_step }`, `EnvQuestion { name, prompt, default }`, `EnvAnswer { name, value }`, the events `LaunchEvent::Refused(Box<Refusal>)` and `LaunchEvent::NeedsInput(Vec<EnvQuestion>)`, and the required method `LaunchFeed::answer_env(Vec<EnvAnswer>)`. A backend that never asks never receives an answer, and a backend that asks and does not implement the method does not compile.
- The cockpit draws a refusal as an error dialog of the feed's four texts (the title, the `found` line, `State`, `Next step`) and nothing else: it is over at once (no Cancel button, no `x`), it creates nothing, and closing it goes to the manager with no instance. It draws no text of its own for it. The page behind reads `Not loading {role} {target}` and `not started`.
- The cockpit opens one form for `NeedsInput`, `Values for this launch` with the role in the border, a field for each question (the prompt as the label, the default pre-filled, `sets {NAME}` as the help, `no default` as the placeholder), `Cancel launch` and `Start launch`. Start sends the answers in the order of the questions to `answer_env`. Esc or `Cancel launch` ends the launch through `LaunchFeed::cancel`, so the backend decides whether it kept a record.
- `Progress` has `ended()` (done, failed, refused or cancelled: nothing to cancel) and `halted()` (ended or a stage blocked: the feed sends nothing more). The old `is_terminal` was the second and was also used as the first, so `x`, the `Cancel launch…` button and the `x` hint were off while a stage was blocked, and the status `This stage is blocked · x cancels the launch` promised a key that did nothing. They now follow `ended()`, and `x` on a blocked launch asks `Cancel the launch?` and ends in `LaunchFeed::cancel`.
- Simulator: `SimBackend::guarded()` and `sim::launch::opening`. Only the guarded simulator checks before the first stage: a workspace whose `RolePolicy` does not allow the role is refused, and the prompted entries of the role's manifest in `Surfaces.roles` are asked. `SimLaunch` holds the launch while it waits for the answers and its `cancel` returns no record then (nothing exists yet). No other simulator changes.
- Fixtures: the scenario `launch-checks` (16 scenarios now). `populated` plus the role manifests, `infra-control-plane` launches `sentinel` (whose manifest prompts for `SENTINEL_FAIL_PREFLIGHT`, default `0`, and `SENTINEL_NOTE`), and `customer-portal` allows only `backend` while its last role is `writer`, so the demo launch-role rule picks a role that workspace does not allow. This is the backstop of v6 4.5: the picker data function never offers such a role, so the view is reached through the guarded simulator.
- `library_has_no_demo.rs` scans for `Role not allowed`, `does not allow role` and `nothing was created` outside `sim/`, `fixtures/` and `bin/`.
- Item 15 needs no library change: the disabled rows and their reasons are B3b and B4c data (`Surfaces.agent_blocked`, `Account.launch_blocked`, `launch_role`). B7b adds the baseline and the journey that proves a disabled row starts nothing.

### Red

1. The blocked-launch tests need no new API, so they ran on the pristine base (`cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test launch_blocked`, `3 tests run: 1 passed, 2 failed`):

```text
x_cancels_a_blocked_launch_and_keeps_its_record          x did nothing      (no `Cancel the launch?` dialog)
keeping_a_blocked_launch_leaves_it_blocked               passes before and after (guard)
```

2. The other tests use the new types, so they do not compile on the base, and a compile error is not a disagreement. The scaffold keeps the old behaviour: the new types and events, `answer_env` as a no-op, the cockpit ignoring `Refused` and `NeedsInput`, the scenario `launch-checks` with the same world data as the final code but a plain `SimBackend`, and `SimBackend::guarded()` returning the plain simulator. Command: `cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --all-features --test launch_blocked --test launch_checks --test launch_feed --test library_has_no_demo --test entry --no-fail-fast`, exit 100, `63 tests run: 51 passed, 12 failed`. The red lines:

```text
the_feeds_questions_open_a_form_and_the_answers_go_back     never reached: the questions

the_sim_refuses_a_role_the_workspace_does_not_allow         never reached: the refusal

the_guarded_sim_sends_the_refusal_before_any_stage          left: [StageChanged(0, Running), Activity("Resolving launch identity…")]
                                                            right: [Refused(Refusal { title: "Role not allowed", found: "Workspace customer-portal does not allow role writer.", ... })]
the_guarded_sim_holds_the_launch_until_the_questions_are_answered   left: []   right: [[EnvQuestion { name: "SENTINEL_FAIL_PREFLIGHT", ... }, EnvQuestion { name: "SENTINEL_NOTE", ... }]]
cancelling_the_guarded_sim_at_the_questions_keeps_no_record  left: Some(Instance { id: "jk-b5df", ... status: FailedSetup ... })   right: None
```

The rule test of `library_has_no_demo.rs` with the three new rules removed: `the_cockpit_draws_no_refusal_of_its_own`, `Role not allowed in screens/cockpit.rs: []   left: 0   right: 1`. The guards `a_backend_that_does_not_guard_neither_asks_nor_refuses`, `the_sim_launches_a_role_the_workspace_allows_and_asks_nothing` and `a_role_without_questions_starts_at_once` pass on the scaffold and on the final code.

3. The journeys, on the scaffold with no baseline files (`--test functional -E 'test(/jackin_fixed_launch_picker|jackin_launch_refuses|jackin_launch_asks|jackin_launch_questions/)'`, `4 tests run: 0 passed, 4 failed`):

### Named breaks

Each break was applied to the finished code, `launch_blocked`, `launch_checks`, `launch_feed` and `library_has_no_demo` were run, and the file was restored with `git checkout`.

| Break | Red |
|---|---|
| `ask_cancel` returns when the launch is `halted()` instead of `ended()` | `x_cancels_a_blocked_launch_and_keeps_its_record`, `a_blocked_stage_keeps_the_cancel_it_offers` |
| the refusal dialog title is a literal `Role not allowed` instead of `r.title` | `real_tree_is_clean` (the scan; the scripted refusal test also shows the title in the activity line, so it does not catch this alone) |
| the form's answers are the defaults, not the typed values | `the_feeds_questions_open_a_form_and_the_answers_go_back` |
| Esc on the form clears the questions and does not call `cancel` | `escape_on_the_questions_cancels_the_launch_and_creates_nothing`, `escape_on_the_sim_questions_records_nothing` |
| `opening` skips the allow-list check | `the_sim_refuses_a_role_the_workspace_does_not_allow`, `the_guarded_sim_sends_the_refusal_before_any_stage` |
| `SimLaunch::cancel` keeps a record while it waits for answers | `cancelling_the_guarded_sim_at_the_questions_keeps_no_record`, `escape_on_the_sim_questions_records_nothing` |
| every `SimBackend` rule runs `opening` (not only `guarded`) | `jackin_launch_openshell_stages` (`timed out: visible stage 5 of 7 · Upload`: the approved `launch-openshell` launch of `sentinel` in `payments-platform` would be refused) |

For the reviewer: the picker cuts the reason of a disabled row (`usage only · jackin launches Codex on…`, the 39-character reason in a 38-column detail cell), and `Picker` reveals a cut detail only for enabled rows, so the full reason is not reachable by key or hover. The baseline records the cut. The form shows `optional` on every field because no question is required.

### Deviations

1. Item 15 has no library change; only the baseline and the journey are new (the data is B3b and B4c). A click on a disabled row starts nothing, which the journey asserts.
2. Items 15 and 16 are named in v6 `jackin_fixed_launch_picker` and `jackin_role_not_allowed`; the env prompt is `jackin_launch_env_prompt` (v6 gives it no name).
3. The refusal and the questions are reached through a new scenario, `launch-checks`, and a new simulator rule, `SimBackend::guarded()`. The picker's launch-role function never offers a role the workspace does not allow, so no existing world reaches the backstop, and a check applied to every simulator would refuse the approved `launch-openshell` launch.
4. `x` on a blocked launch asks `Cancel the launch?` first, like `x` on a running one, instead of cancelling at once.
5. In a blocked cockpit `Enter` opens the build log (the `b | Enter` arm precedes the `Esc | Enter if blocked` arm), so only `Esc` shows the status `This stage is blocked · x cancels the launch`. Not changed.
6. The `x Cancel launch…` hint is cut by the footer width in the hard-cases world (its persistent status takes the room), so the hint is asserted on the scripted feed and the button on the hard-cases world.
7. `Next step` of the refusal can be cut in a dialog: `InfoDialog` ignores `Prop::wrap`. The refusal dialog is 80 wide so the v6 text fits whole.

It now detaches first, and passed in the three runs after.

## B8-1: TerminalViewport, selection, copy, the track column, ViewportOps (BX26, G20)

Plan: the approved P9.3 design (v7.1) section 6.7 and the B8-1 row of 7.2, and test plan (v7.1) T42 with review amendments N10, N11 and N25, plus the B8-1 half of T48 (`tests/pane_edge.rs`). Tests: `tests/terminal_viewport.rs` and `tests/pane_edge.rs` (root crate, `-p junie-tui`, feature `terminal`), and the `showcase_terminal_viewport_*` journeys and `showcase_page_terminalviewport` in `tests/functional/showcase.rs`. The tests were written before the code and run against 93949cf first, in a clean worktree of that commit.

Reproduce one record:

```sh
cargo nextest run --locked --no-fail-fast -p junie-tui --all-features --test <file> -E 'test(=<name>)'
cargo nextest run --locked --no-fail-fast -p junie-tui --all-features --test functional -E 'test(=showcase::<name>)'
```

### Red on 93949cf

None of the new types exists on 93949cf, so the two library test files fail to compile (exit 101):

```text
error[E0432]: unresolved import `junie_tui::terminal::TerminalViewport`
 --> tests/pane_edge.rs:6:42
error[E0432]: unresolved import `junie_tui::terminal::TerminalViewport`
 --> tests/terminal_viewport.rs:7:53
error: could not compile `junie-tui` (test "pane_edge") due to 1 previous error
error: could not compile `junie-tui` (test "terminal_viewport") due to 1 previous error
```

The twelve showcase tests fail because the page does not exist (exit 100, `Summary [1.843s] 12 tests run: 0 passed, 12 failed, 96 skipped`). Every one of them stops with the same two lines, the first from the binary and the second from `App::wait_text`:

```text
error: invalid value 'terminalviewport' for '--page <NAME>': unknown page "terminalviewport"
application exited while visible Terminal viewport: Some(2)
```

A compile error and a missing page show only that the API is missing. The named breaks below show that each test fails on the implemented code when one behaviour is wrong.

### Green

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p junie-tui --all-features --test terminal_viewport --test pane_edge   exit 0   30 tests run: 30 passed, 0 skipped
cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p junie-tui --all-features --test functional -E 'test(/terminal_viewport|terminalviewport/)'   exit 0   12 tests run: 12 passed (five runs in a row, load average above 30)
```

No approved baseline moved: `git diff --name-status 93949cf -- tests/functional/baselines apps/jackin/tests/functional/baselines` lists six `A` lines and nothing else. The two baselines that render through the code B8-1 touched in `src/terminal.rs` (`TerminalView::render` now calls the shared `paint_view`), `showcase_page_terminalpane` and `showcase_terminal_replay_mid_sequence`, pass unchanged.

### New baselines

Each was accepted with `tuiscotti accept --candidate <sample> --candidate-sha256 <sha> --expect-baseline missing --baselines tests/functional/baselines` after the candidate was rendered to PNG and read. They need `ux-specialist` review before the merge (D27, design 7.2).

| Baseline | What it shows |
| --- | --- |
| `showcase_page_terminalviewport` | The page at rest, 120x40. A 38x12 grid in a framed pane with the last column as the scroll track (dashed track, thumb on the bottom four rows), 22 rows of history, a 75-character path that the shell wrapped over three rows, the prompt, `live` in the pane meta, the state list (`grid 38x12 + track`, `scroll live tail`, `history 22 rows`, caret row 12 col 19) and the `Feed line` and `Reset` buttons. The pane has no focus, so no hardware cursor. |
| `showcase_terminal_viewport_focused` | The same page after a click in the pane, a wheel up and `End`: the pane is focused (bright title, hints `PgUp PgDn Scroll`, `Home Oldest`, `End Live tail`, `Esc Clear selection`) and the hardware cursor rests after the prompt (checked in the test against the prompt cell). |
| `showcase_terminal_viewport_scrolled` | One wheel notch up (three rows): pane meta `20–31 of 34`, `scroll 3 rows up`, the thumb moved off the bottom, the first and last rows faded (more above, more below), no hardware cursor. |
| `showcase_terminal_viewport_selection` | A drag over the wrapped path, from the `/` to the end of its third row: the highlight covers only the selected cells of each physical row, the state list reads `selection 75 characters` and `copied 1 line · 75 characters` (the three rows joined with no newline), the status bar reads `Copied 75 characters · /home/dev/projects/jackin/crates/jackin…`. |
| `showcase_terminal_viewport_fullscreen` | The `fullscreen` fixture: an alternate-screen program (a box and a status cell) with the cursor hidden. `alt screen on`, `caret hidden`, the track column is drawn at rest with no thumb, because the alternate screen has no history to reach (the 9 rows of primary history stay out of reach). |
| `showcase_terminal_viewport_states` | The States tab: three samples in the library state grid, 6 rows each, `rest` (live tail, thumb at the bottom), `empty` (no history, the track at rest without a thumb) and `overflow` (scrolled back, first and last rows faded). |

### Decisions and deviations

1. The page is hidden (`HIDDEN_ENTRIES`), because a sidebar row would change the `Components 54` count and the sidebar of every showcase baseline (D27). `docs/audits/2026-11-04-terminal-viewport-showcase-page.md` records it.
2. `TerminalViewport` owns its `TerminalState` (`new(id, state)`, `feed`, `resize`, `reset`). `ViewportOps` has no state argument, so a viewport that reads a grid must hold it. `state()` gives a shared reference only, so the offset and the selection cannot be left stale by a caller.
3. The selection ends at the pointer the way `TextViewport` does: the head is the left edge of the cell under the pointer. T42 says "from column 5 of row 0 to column 2 of row 1 copies `FGHIJKLM`"; the tests drag to the boundary after `M` (x=3) and copy the same text. A press or a head inside the second half of a wide character moves to the character.
4. `keyboard_copy()` is false; `End` is `set_follow(true)`, `Esc` is `clear_selection`, a click is `on_click`. The viewport has no key handler (a key belongs to the program), so these three T42 cases are tested through the ops, and the showcase page maps the keys (`showcase_terminal_viewport_keys_page_and_clear`).
5. The wheel moves the number of rows it is given. The 3-row notch is `PointerConfig::wheel_lines` (`src/core/pointer.rs:87`, N25); the tests send `-3`.
6. "Drag release copies" is host wiring (the viewport has no release op). The page copies on `Phase::DragEnd` and on a double click, and `showcase_terminal_viewport_copy_joins_a_wrapped_path` proves the copied text and the status.
7. A scrolled-back view fades its first and last rows through `ui::fade::scroll_edges`; the live tail does not. `TextViewport` fades whenever there is history, which at the tail would dim the first row of every program that fills the screen.
8. The viewport maps the program's default foreground and background to the theme (`text_primary` and the `bg` the host passes); a program colour stays as it is, and a mono theme drops colours. `TerminalView` keeps the terminal defaults (`Color::Reset`), so its two baselines are unchanged.
9. Text of `selected_text` is `None` when the range holds no characters, so a copy of blank cells is not an empty clipboard write.
10. Not tested by a named break: `mouse_mode_does_not_change_scrolling` (the viewport does not read the mouse mode, so no one-edit break turns it red; it pins the contract for B8b, which forwards the wheel as arrow keys on the alternate screen only) and the `&self` of `draw`, which the compiler enforces.
11. T48 `below_floor_note` is the B8b half (the `Pane too small` state is the pane's, not the viewport's) and is not in this step.
12. `src/widgets/mod.rs` makes `text_select` `pub(crate)` so `terminal` can reuse `TextSelect` and `is_word`; nothing becomes public.
13. `ViewportOps` leaves the catalog allowlist: the page draws, scrolls, selects and copies a viewport through the trait.

### B8-1 gate

Run on the tree that holds the commits, each command separately:

```text
cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 --workspace --all-targets --all-features                    exit 0   504 tests run: 504 passed, 1 skipped
cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 -p jackin-console --no-default-features --features product  exit 0   27 tests run: 27 passed, 0 skipped
cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings               exit 0
cargo fmt --all --check                                                                     exit 0
git diff --name-status 93949cf -- tests/functional/baselines apps/jackin/tests/functional/baselines   no output (the six baselines are new files; `git ls-files --others` lists them until the commit, `git diff --name-status 93949cf <commit>` lists six A lines)
```

The skipped test is the quarantined `tablepro` write gate. The red worktree of 93949cf was removed after the red runs.

### Named breaks (41, every one red)

Each break is one exact text replacement in `src/terminal/viewport.rs`, applied and reverted by a script Each ran with the filter of its tests.
