## PTY load flakes

Decision D27 holds: no approved baseline changes. `git status --porcelain` on
`tests/functional/baselines` and `apps/jackin/tests/functional/baselines` is
empty before and after the work.

Symptom: under machine load (five parallel cargo jobs) a full
`cargo nextest run --locked -j 4 --workspace --all-targets --all-features` failed
`jackin::jackin_roles_add_role` (2.5 s) and
`showcase::showcase_terminal_viewport_double_click_selects_a_word` (2.0 s; three
of four full runs on the P9.3 integration branch, which quarantined it). Both
passed 5 of 5 alone. `tablepro_write_confirmation_cancels_retries_and_executes_only_when_armed`
was already quarantined in "PTY torn frames". The run log named in the brief
held no failure text, so both failures were reproduced.

### Reproduction

CPU burners are `while :; do :; done` subshells started by one script that
records their PIDs and stops them by PID (no `pkill`). The machine was also
loaded by other sessions (load average 45 to 120 before the burners).
`cargo nextest run --stress-count N -E 'test(...)'` repeats one test.

| Test | Burners | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `showcase_terminal_viewport_double_click_selects_a_word` | 24 | 3 of 20 failed | 0 of 100 |
| `jackin_roles_add_role` | 48 | 1 of 60 failed (0 of 20 at 24 burners) | 0 of 60 |
| `tablepro_write_confirmation_cancels_retries_and_executes_only_when_armed` (run with `--run-ignored all` before) | 24 | 8 of 30 failed | 0 of 30 |
| whole `functional` target (158 journeys), 3 nextest runs per iteration | 24 | one pass before: 154 of 154 (the target then held 154 journeys) | 0 of 20 iterations failed (3160 journey runs) |

"Before" is `2206bba` (the integration branch with the double-click quarantine).
The whole-target rate before is a single pass, so it does not separate the trees;
the per-test rates carry the proof.

### First wrong state

1. Double click. The failure is the last assertion, not the wait
   (`tests/functional/showcase.rs`, `status_row(&selected)` contains
   `Copied 8 characters · finished`). The saved checkpoint `003.txt` of a failed
   run shows `selection   8 characters` and `copied      nothing yet`. A word
   selects on the second press; the copy and its status come on the second
   release (`src/bin/showcase/pages/terminal_viewport.rs`, `released && let Some(text)`).
   `click` is four separate PTY writes and returns at once. The wait matched the
   frame the app drew after press two, and the test then read the status from that
   same frame. The release was still in the PTY.
2. `jackin_roles_add_role`. The failure is `jackin.rs` `assert!(text(&invalid).contains("Use a directory such as"))`.
   The frame shows the Add role form with `roles/new-role` typed and nothing else:
   `submit_location` had written `enter tab tab enter`, and `settle()` returned
   before the app read them. `settle()` is `wait_quiet` for 150 ms of silence. A
   descheduled app is silent too.
3. Write gate (already diagnosed in "PTY torn frames"): the status lifetime
   (`StatusLine::DEFAULT_TTL_MS`, 5 s) runs on `WallClock` in tablepro, so the
   hidden cursor of the frozen frame depends on whether the flash expired before
   `No open tabs` was drawn.

The first hypothesis, that the double-click interval was measured in wall time and
the two clicks fell outside it, was tested and is not the cause of the reported
failure: the double click registered (the word was selected). The interval is
still wall time (see "Not done").

### Why the structure allowed it

Nothing in the harness could tell when the app had handled an input. Every
input method returned after a `write(2)` to the PTY; every later read was a
snapshot of whatever the app had drawn so far. Waits and `settle()` therefore
passed on any frame that happened to satisfy them, including frames from before
the input. 15 wait predicates in "PTY torn frames" and the two failures here are
the same shape: the assertion is about a later state than the wait. Under load the
window between the write and the read of the app is the size of a scheduling delay.

The write gate adds a second input to the same structure: an app timer on wall
time while the journey runs on its own clock. Jackin and holla already keep a
simulated clock that only `advance_clock` moves.

### Fix

- `src/clock.rs`, `src/shell/app.rs`, `src/shell/clipboard.rs`: `ClockStep`
  becomes `TestRequest { Advance(ms), Fence }`. Under `JUNIE_TUI_TEST_CLOCK=1`
  the shell answers the paste `junie-tui:fence` with a BEL, queued on the
  existing output queue, so it is written after the frame of every earlier input.
  The fence returns before status, refusal and pointer state are touched, so it
  changes nothing the journey can see. `Shell::clock_steps` is now `test_requests`.
- `tests/functional/support.rs`: `fence()` pastes the request, reads the
  emulator's bell count before and after, and waits for it to rise. It runs
  first in `screen()` and `settle()`, so every wait predicate, `stable_screen`,
  `try_sustained` and `visual` read a screen that shows all earlier input as
  handled. Writes stay as asynchronous as before. Nothing sleeps for a fixed time.
- `WallClock::for_run` (`src/clock.rs`), used by tablepro: under the same opt-in
  the status `Moment` (`Clock::now`) is a value that only `advance` moves;
  `now_ms`, which the pointer gestures use, stays on wall time. tablepro
  implements `advance_clock` with `clock::step_wall`, which ticks the app at its
  own interval, so the expiry runs as it does in real time.
- Tests that waited for real time now step it: the write gate, `freeze`, and the
  history wait in `tablepro.rs` call `advance_clock(5_000)`. The write gate lost
  its `#[ignore]`. The double-click journey lost its `#[ignore]`.
- `holla_first_use_idle_and_no_results`: the fence exposed that its wait
  (`!contains("cargo check")`) held on the frame before the query was typed, and
  its assertion `contains("Search actions and resources")` was true only of that
  frame. The wait is now for `Nothing to preview`, and the test asserts the typed
  query is shown and the placeholder is gone. This changes what the test asserts
  from a state the app has left to the state the app is in. It needs the reviewer's
  look. The baseline `holla_first_use_idle` is unchanged.

### What was tried and dropped

A fence after every write (the first version) made the app handle a key and the
next mouse move in separate frames. `showcase_tree_expand_and_file_selection`
has a frozen baseline whose cursor is the end of the last diff, taken with the two
in one batch, and it failed 19 of 20. The fence moved to the read side; that test
passed 60 of 60 and 29 of 30 under 24 burners (the unmodified tree: 60 of 60).

Freezing every timer of showcase and tablepro (the pointer clock too) broke 15
journeys, for three reasons: the press flash (140 ms) and the refusal flash never
end, the hover dwell of tooltips (600 ms) never ends, and every press at one cell is
a double click. A harness that paces the clock per gesture broke jackin and holla,
whose simulated clock the journeys already drive by hand. So only the status clock
of tablepro is stepped.

showcase stays on the wall clock for its status too. With a stepped showcase status,
`showcase_tree_expand_and_file_selection` failed 7 of 10 on a cursor mismatch
(`cursor` at row 9, column 79 against the frozen row 39, column 93). I did not
find why a status time changes the last frame of a paused page. This is open.

### Not done

- The double-click interval, the press flash and the hover dwell of showcase and
  tablepro are still wall time. A load that holds the app for 500 ms between two
  presses would still break a double click. 0 of 100 failed at 24 burners.
- The cursor of a frozen frame is still the end of the last diff written (see
  "PTY torn frames"). Parking it would move approved baselines.
- `showcase_tree_expand_and_file_selection` and the showcase status line: see above.
  The test is still order sensitive: its frozen cursor needs the footer to be the
  last write (1 failure in 90 runs under burners with the read fence).
- One unexplained fence timeout: `holla_resize_finder_recovers` did not see the BEL
  within 10 s in 1 of 158 journeys in a quiet pass, and in 30 of 30 repeats. Cause
  not found; the failure message now names the bell counts.
- `jackin_terminal_detached_exited_and_failed_panes` failed once in 60 journeys
  with the write fence (the Error status "Could not attach" was gone: the clock
  request is a paste, and a paste clears an Error status). With the read fence it
  passed 30 of 30. A clock request still counts as user input for the status line.

### Test plan for test-reviewer

No test was written. A regression test for the fence: a functional journey that
reads the screen 200 times after a key and a click pair and asserts the bell count
rose once per read, red when `shell::handle` drops the `Fence` branch
(the helper then times out waiting for the BEL). A regression test for the clock:
the write gate itself (red on `2206bba` 8 of 30, green 0 of 30).

### PTY load flakes: tree journey quarantine

Quarantined 2026-11-04 after the read-fence merge: `showcase::showcase_tree_expand_and_file_selection` failed once in a loaded full run (739 of 740). Cause, from the harness fix: its frozen cursor needs the footer to be the last write of the frame, and under load the status line can be drawn after it. The fix is to step the showcase status clock the way tablepro's is stepped (it failed 7 of 10 when tried, cause not yet found) or to park the hidden cursor; it removes this `#[ignore]` and proves 20 of 20 under burners.
